## Document 020: 20260930_投資週報_經濟動能穩健，延續科技輪動行情

Source PDF: all report pdf/20260930_投資週報_經濟動能穩健，延續科技輪動行情.pdf
Report source: Yuanta (投資週報)
Date: 2026-09-30
Pages: 1
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同
意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 9 月 30 日
上週重點訊息
◼ 美國企業投資動能維持韌性 ：8 月標普全球美國綜合 PMI 升至 58.4，創2021 年 7 月來高點 ，
製造業、服務業同步擴張至 57 和 58.7； 8 月扣除飛機與國防的核心資本財訂單月增 1.6%，
高於前月修正後的 0.6%。其中，初級金屬、機械及電機設備訂單均呈現成長，顯示企業資本
支出、AI 投資與國防需求仍具韌性，持續支撐美國經濟成長。
◼ 美中同意貿易休兵再延長兩個月：美中同意調降雙邊總值 600 億美元商品的關稅，涵蓋美國

玉米、化妝品及中國家電、玩具等產品，並延長貿易休兵至明年 1 月 10 日，同時中國承諾繼
續供應稀土並擴大採購美國農產品與煤礦，美國則繼續暫停實施 50%穿透性規則；AI 方面，
美中雙方雖未就 AI 監管議題達成共識 ， 但同意建立風險溝通機制 ， 並於11 月底前展開對話 。
◼ Meta 旗下 AI 代理人 Muse 登頂排行榜：Meta 新款個人 AI 代理 Muse 上線後迅速累積逾
250 萬次下載，並登上美國 App Store 下載排行榜首位。相較過去生成式 AI 以問答與內容生
成為主，Muse 可進一步協助安排日程、搜尋資訊、處理文件及完成交易，代表 AI 正從提升
效率工具逐步走向數位勞動力，市場評價焦點也將逐漸由 AI 基礎設施擴散至應用軟體業者。
市場展望
◼ AI 需求持續擴張，科技供應鏈成長動能延續：AI 基礎建設需求持續擴張，帶動 HBM、伺服器
DRAM、 先進製程與封裝等關鍵供應鏈維持成長動能 ， 市場表現由全面上漲逐步轉向結構性分
化。若 AI 伺服器需求持續成長且 CSP 資本支出維持高檔，具供給優勢、定價能力及較高訂單

能見度之企業，其營運表現有機會獲得支撐。整體而言，科技產業獲利表現仍具關注度，惟後
續仍須留意全球景氣、產業供需變化、政策監管及市場評價調整等風險因素。
◼ 債券佈局可關注中天期美國公債及投等債：中天期美國公債及投資級債仍值得持續關注：**雖
然貨幣政策風險下降有助於穩定長期通膨預期 ， 但政府與企業發債供給增加所帶來的排擠效應 ，
以及美國經濟成長展現韌性，仍可能使長天期利率面臨上行壓力。因此，在當前利率環境下，
中天期債券於票息收益與存續期間風險之間相對較為均衡。投資級債方面，彭博美國投資級公
司債指數利差約 80 個基點，雖然利差偏窄，但高基準利率環境仍提供相對較高的收益水準。
因此，在目前市場環境下，中天期美國公債及投資級債仍為值得持續觀察的債券資產類別，惟
後續表現仍須留意利率走勢、信用利差變化及經濟成長動能等因素。

## Document 026: 20261027_投資週報_AI資金壓力擾動市場，科技基本面仍穩健

Source PDF: all report pdf/20261027_投資週報_AI資金壓力擾動市場，科技基本面仍穩健.pdf
Report source: Yuanta (投資週報)
Date: 2026-10-27
Pages: 1
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同
意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 10 月 27 日
上週重點訊息
◼ AI 投資回報疑慮升溫，資金轉向債市避險：市場對大型科技企業 AI 基礎建設投資規模、融資
需求及未來獲利回收能力的疑慮升溫，導致科技股賣壓擴大，10 月 22 日 Nasdaq 指數下跌
約 2.6%。 隨著市場風險偏好下降 ， 避險資金轉向美國公債 ，10 年期公債殖利率單日下降約 19
個基點，創 2023 年 3 月以來最大單日跌幅，並於 23 日回升至 4.99%。市場關注焦點逐漸由
AI 投資規模轉向資本支出效益，後續大型科技企業財報及現金流表現，將成為評估 AI 產業評

價合理性的重要依據。
◼ 中東衝突推升通膨風險：中東衝突持續升溫，伊朗支持的葉門胡塞武裝攻擊沙烏地阿拉伯，引
發市場對原油供應中斷的擔憂，推升布蘭特原油價格於10 月 23 日升至每桶 116.72 美元，單
週上漲約 6.2%，創 2022 年以來最高週收盤水準。能源價格攀升加劇全球通膨壓力，並壓抑
市場風險偏好。
◼ 法國政治動盪加劇財政疑慮，歐洲主權債風險重新受到關注：法國因生活成本上升爆發大規模
抗議，進一步加深市場對政府財政整頓能力的疑慮。10 月 19 日法國 10 年期公債殖利率上升
約 10 個基點至 4.88%附近，與德國公債利差擴大至約 144 個基點，創 2012 年以來最高收盤
水準 ， 法股單日下跌約1.5%， 歐元同步走弱 。 市場擔憂政治不確定性可能削弱財政改革進度 ，
並透過主權債評價影響銀行業資產負債表。後續須關注法國財政政策、歐洲主權債利差及銀行
融資成本變化。
市場展望
◼ 科技股短線宜審慎，中期聚焦基本面穩健的 AI 企業：近期 AI 資本支出、融資需求及評價調整

使科技股波動加劇 ， 但先進製程與半導體設備需求仍具韌性 ，AI 中長期成長趨勢未改變 。 隨市
場焦點由成長題材轉向投資效益，企業營收與訂單能見度、自由現金流及資本效率的重要性提
升 ， 具技術優勢 、 財務穩健及獲利能見度的企業相對有利 ， 而高度依賴外部融資者宜審慎看待 。
◼ 美債避險功能重現，中天期債券風險報酬相對均衡：市場風險偏好降溫時，美國公債仍具一定
避險效果。儘管 Fed 貨幣政策路徑與能源價格增添利率不確定性，短天期債券易受政策利率
牽動，超長天期債券面臨財政赤字、國債供給及期限溢價等壓力，相較之下，中天期美債的風
險報酬較為均衡。信用債方面，投資等級企業基本面及現金流仍具韌性，後續仍可持續關注。

市場焦點
美國
公債回購
• 美國財政部9月9日宣布，將於翌日回購最高60億美元的10至20年期美債，低於部分市場期待
的80億至100億美元規模 。民間持有美債規模達 31.8兆美元，相較之下 ，本次60億美元回購
僅約占民間持債規模 0.02%，對整體影響有限 。此外，約6成的美債期限不足 5年，意味未來
再融資需求仍高，若財政赤字未明顯收斂，長端殖利率上行壓力仍存。
日本央行
• 日本第二季GDP年化季增率上修至 1.4%(優於初值1.1%)，儘管伊朗戰爭引發原油供應緊張給
經濟帶來壓力，但GDP增速依舊高於0.7%潛在經濟成長率，雖然經濟表現支持日本央行(BOJ)
逐步實施貨幣政策正常化 ，但更關鍵的催化劑來自日美兩國協調干預匯市 ，遏制日圓極端貶
值的政治壓力，彭博經濟研究預期日本央行將在9月會議上加息25個基點。

美國
通膨
• 美國8月生產者物價指數 （PPI）年增5.4%，略高市場預期的 5.3%，核心PPI年增4.6%，符合
預期。8月消費者物價指數（CPI）年增3.35%、核心CPI年增2.4%，均符合市場預期，但核心
CPI月升幅0.3%，高於市場預期的 0.2%。數據公佈後，FedWatch顯示市場預期9月升息機率，
從前一周的50%攀升至86%。

美國8月通膨數據(CPI)
• 美國8月通膨數據：8月美國CPI月增0.4%、年增3.4%，核心CPI月增0.3%，核心服務通膨升溫至0.5%，顯示通膨降溫仍不
均衡，能源價格上漲為主要推手，市場預估8月核心PCE月增約0.3%、年增約3.8%，仍高於Fed 2%目標，搭配強勁就業數
據，9月升息1碼的壓力升高。不過，本次通膨主要來自能源、需求及AI投資帶動，與2021～2022年供給型通膨有所不同。
• 市場反應與展望：CPI公布後，2年期美債殖利率一度上升，惟長天期殖利率反而走低，顯示市場關注焦點並非單純升息，而
是Fed能否維持抗通膨政策可信度。整體而言，升息未必等同Risk-off，若經濟與企業獲利維持韌性，股市仍具支撐。
資料來源： investing.com，2026.9.14

能源成本回升推升PPI，通膨壓力牽動Fed政策空間
• 能源成本回升，PPI重新升溫：美國8月PPI月增0.4%、年增5.4%，增幅均明顯高於7月，主要受到商品價格上漲帶動；其
中最終需求商品價格月增1.1%，能源價格上升4.2%，柴油更是大漲逾2成，成為商品價格上升的重要來源，惟最終需求服
務價格僅略增0.1%，顯示本次PPI升溫仍以能源及商品價格為主。
• 成本壓力仍在，利率政策不確定性升高： 扣除食品、能源及貿易服務後的核心 PPI月增0.3%，微低於7月的0.4%，但年增
4.7%，顯示價格壓力仍未完全消退；其中運輸與倉儲服務價格上升2.3%，反映部分成本仍具黏著性，致使市場對Fed利率
政策的重新定價明顯升溫，進而推動股、債市短線波動擴大。
• 市場觀察：由於本次PPI升溫主要來自能源成本 ，但服務價格壓力整體仍相對溫和 ，因此尚不足以視為通膨全面升溫 ，然

能源價格若持續走高 ，將提高Fed維持較高利率的壓力 ，建議投資人短線宜降低長天期債券與高估值成長股的追價 ，債券
可優先配置短中天期、高品質債券，股票則可關注能源、金融及具定價能力企業，提高投組對利率與成本波動的承受度。
資料來源：U.S. Bureau of Labor Statistics，Producer Price Indexes – Aug,  2026，2026.9.11

• 財政部對美債的回購規模不如預期：美國財政部9月9日宣布，將於翌日回購最高60億美元的10至20年期美債，主要鎖定流

動性較低的舊券，以改善長天期債券市場流動性。雖然本次回購高於先前設定的每次至少40億美元，但低於部分市場期待的
80億至100億美元規模。消息公布後10年期公債殖利率逼近5%，30年期殖利率亦突破5.3%。
• 回購規模在高額赤字背景下，難以有效拉低長天期公債殖利率：美國今年公債發行規模較2025年增加11.8%，民間持有美債
規模達31.8兆美元、年增8.2%，相較之下，本次60億美元回購僅約占民間持債規模0.02%，對整體影響有限。此外，約6成
的美債期限不足5年，意味未來再融資需求仍高，若財政赤字未明顯收斂，長端殖利率上行壓力仍存。反觀新興國家基本面
持續改善，且當地債市有貨幣潛在升值空間，可作為全球債券配置的分散來源。

• 地緣政治動盪推升油價，但全球經濟依然展現韌性：美國和伊朗的衝突已經進入第7個月，能源價格上升及地緣政治風險仍
對全球經濟與市場信心形成壓力，但AI投資熱潮持續推升科技需求，使全球經濟展現優於預期的韌性。其中，亞洲仍是全球
成長動能最強的區域之一，2026年亞洲（不含日本）經濟成長率預估達4.7%，明顯高於全球平均2.9%及成熟市場1.6%。在
內需與科技出口支撐下，新興亞洲基本面相對穩健，具備較佳的中長期配置條件。

• AI資本支出延續，台韓成為核心受惠市場：AI資本支出持續擴張，2026年全球雲端AI資本支出預估達7,960億美元，並帶動
AI半導體市場至2030年突破7,500億美元。亞洲掌握全球半導體供應鏈核心，台灣受惠於先進製程與先進封裝需求，韓國則
受惠於HBM記憶體需求，台韓可望成為AI基礎建設擴張的主要受惠市場；同時，中國與印度則提供較穩定的內需及經濟成
長支撐。2026年亞洲企業盈餘成長率預估達72.13%，顯著高於全球平均，建議新興亞洲以台韓科技股為主要成長配置，並
搭配中國、印度市場分散區域風險。

短債供給增加未引發融資緊張，短天期債券防禦價值提升
• 回購市場維持穩定，短債供給暫未造成壓力：美國回購市場今年以來維持平穩，儘管9月短債償付與企業繳稅可能帶來短
暫資金波動，但SOFR與IORB利差仍處相對正常區間 ，顯示目前準備金與短期流動性尚稱充裕 。此外SOFR/IORB差值對
短期美債淨發行增加的敏感度明顯低於2025年底，反映流動性環境偏寬鬆。
• 貨幣基金成長放緩，供給吸收能力受到限制：零售資金流入偏弱，使貨幣市場基金資產增速落後 ，2026年迄今僅增加約
325億美元，遠低於2025年同期的2,050億美元；即便Q4季節性回升，新增資金對短債供給的承接能力仍可能有限。
• 後市觀察：年底前短債淨發行規模預估約3,250億美元，後續需關注短債供給、企業繳稅及財政部長端公債回購對資金面

的影響。建議投資人配置上可提高 1～3年期美國國庫券或短天期美債 ETF比重，以取得票息收益並降低長端殖利率波動
對債券價格的影響。

風險偏好升溫與債券供給增加，推升期限溢價與長債殖利率
• 全球期限溢酬上升，長債面臨重新定價：全球債券殖利率同步走高，不僅反映各國政策利率預期，也受到全球期限溢酬上升
推動；投資人對持有長天期政府債券要求更高補償 ，顯示長債殖利率上行已具有跨市場的結構性因素 。Bloomberg
Economics的模型亦顯示，全球期限溢酬是近年G7長債殖利率走高的重要共同因素。
• 風險資產吸引資金，放大長債供需壓力：AI投資題材推升股票市場、信用利差仍相對偏窄，加上大宗商品走強，使股票、公
司債及商品與政府債券形成資金競爭；在政府融資需求持續增加的情況下 ，若債券需求未同步擴張，政府公債需提供更高殖
利率才能吸引資金。
• 後市觀察：長端利率已成為股債配置的重要變數 ，若殖利率因財政供給與風險偏好維持高檔 ，長天期債券及高估值股票均可

能承壓；建議以短中天期美債及高品質投資級公司債為核心 ，待長端殖利率充分反映期限溢酬後，再分批拉長債券存續期間。

日本央行升息箭在弦上，但缺乏連續升息條件
• GDP修正支持升息 ，但關鍵點在配合日本和美國政府遏制日圓疲軟： 日本第二季 GDP年化季增率上修至 1.4%(優於初值
1.1%)，儘管伊朗戰爭引發原油供應緊張給經濟帶來壓力，但GDP增速依舊高於0.7%潛在經濟成長率，雖然經濟表現支持日
本央行(BOJ)逐步實施貨幣政策正常化 ，但更關鍵的催化劑來自日美兩國協調干預匯市 ，遏制日圓極端貶值的政治壓力 ，彭
博經濟研究預期日本央行將在9月會議上加息25個基點。
• 缺乏連續大幅加息的支撐力道： (1)經濟衝擊風險：日圓升值效應已對經濟產生緊縮效果 ，彭博預估可能拖累實際 GDP成長
達0.3%。(2)財政負擔過重：若政策利率升至 1.75%以上，將大幅增加政府利息支出 ，擠壓高市內閣的財政刺激空間 。(3)企

業投資放緩：私人資本支出仍顯疲軟(季比下降0.9%)，顯示經濟復甦基礎尚未完全穩固。
• 小結：日本央行在政治、匯率與經濟基本面交集下 ，9月升息已成定局，但考量財政負擔與全球金融穩定後 ，其後續加息步
伐應偏向謹慎與循序漸進 ，密切關注9月日本央行會議後聲明 ，若央行強調循序漸進而非連續緊縮 ，市場波動相對可控；反
之，若釋放過度鷹派訊號，則需防範市場拋售風險。由於市場資金對日圓套利交易依賴度仍高，日圓任何短期內的急速升值，
都極易觸發全球風險資產的連鎖去槓桿效應。

中國8月通膨回升，惟需求端疲弱
• 8月份 CPI及PPI：消費者物 價指 數 (CPI)月增 0.4%(前月月減 0.1%)、年增 0.8%(前月 0.5%)，生 產者物價指數 (PPI) 月增
0.4%(前月月減0.7%)、年增3.8%(前月3.5%)。通膨走揚主要受到能源及通訊設備成本上升影響。
• 觀點：中國自2023年以來，內需市場面臨房地產崩盤 、青年失業率飆升以及公共債務攀升等困境 。今年年初以來，通膨雖
然由負轉正，不過從細項得知，因國際油價飆升、AI硬體設備面臨漲價壓力，本波通膨回升主要是受到供給端影響。外部成
本壓力恐進一步壓抑中國內需消費，中國要在真正意義上走出通縮環境可能還需要更多官方政策發力。彭博預估，在需求未
出現明顯改善的情境下，中國央行需要近一步放寬貨幣政策，預測年底前可能降息10個基點。

資料來源：Bloomberg，截至2026.9.9。

AI產業獲利亮眼，但需留意變現能力和資本效率
• AI獲利增速領先大盤，銷售成長成為獲利關鍵：AI板塊在2026年第一與第二季分別實現52.8%與59.7%的高獲利成長，受高
基期影響，未來四季度平均獲利成長預計放緩至 34.4%，但仍大幅優於非 AI板塊12.3%。預計2026年第三季至2027年第二
季，AI平均銷售成長率將達 27.2%，為非AI成分股(5%)5倍以上 。隨著未來利潤率擴張放緩 (預計未來 6個季度平均僅提升
1.66%，低於過去12個季度的3.52%)，持續的銷售成長是維持獲利的關鍵。
• AI供應鏈內部發展並不均衡： 半導體最為強勁，2026Q3至2027Q2預期銷售成長65.8%，獲利大增112.1%且淨利潤率大幅
擴張8.86%，為產業鏈核心引擎。雲端巨頭表現穩健，營收與獲利增速維持約26%，基礎設施軟體與硬體也有15%~20%營

收與獲利成長 。但應用端軟體成長相對乏力 ，未來四季度獲利預期增速僅 4.3%與6.5%，表現低於標普500非AI股平均水準。
• 資本強度創新高 ，但自由現金流承壓： AI資本支出佔營收比重自 2024年Q1的8.9%一路攀升至 2026年Q2的20.1%，預計
2026年下半年將達到23%的高點。資本支出激增導致自由現金流與資本支出之比(FCF/Capex)從2.48倍滑落至0.69倍，自由
現金流利潤率亦從21%降至13.9%。隨著現金逐漸消耗，企業可能需透過債券市場進行發債融資 ，以維持龐大基礎設施投資。
• 小結與投資建議：AI板塊依然是美股獲利驅動力，但隨利潤率提升空間受限、自由現金流受資本支出壓制，投資邏輯已從全
面暴漲轉向注重變現能力與資本效率。投資建議聚焦上游硬體與半導體，建議持續優選具備高定價能力與實質訂單支撐的半
導體與雲端基礎設施龍頭，留意資本支出佔比過高且自由現金流急劇惡化公司，優先選擇現金流充沛、融資成本低企業。
標普500指數盈利成長：AI vs. 非AI
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.7

標普500指數剔除AI後利潤率或下降

• 美國8月通膨數據：8月美國CPI月增0.4%、年增3.4%，核心CPI月增0.3%，核心服務通膨升溫至0.5%，顯示通膨降溫仍不均衡，
能源價格上漲為主要推手，市場預估8月核心PCE月增約0.3%、年增約3.8%，仍高於Fed 2%目標，搭配強勁就業數據，9月升息
1碼的壓力升高。不過，本次通膨主要來自能源、需求及AI投資帶動，與2021～2022年供給型通膨有所不同。
• 市場反應與展望：CPI公布後，2年期美債殖利率一度上升，惟長天期殖利率反而走低，顯示市場關注焦點並非單純升息，而是

Fed能否維持抗通膨政策可信度。整體而言，升息未必等同Risk-off，若經濟與企業獲利維持韌性，股市仍具支撐。

### Page 3

and mSAP demand expansion
While most investors have focused primarily on ABF substrate opportunities over the
past 6+ months, we believe BT substrate products will also benefit from a multi-year
pricing uptrend. Incorporating our revised BT substrate TAM growth and a more
constructive outlook for the mSAP industry, we raise our TPs for ZDT/Kinsus/NYPCB to
NT$1,025/1,515/2,700 (from NT$925/1,125/2,500), supported by 1%-8%/7%-14%
upward revisions to our 2027/28E earnings forecasts. For Unimicron, as the company is

modestly from NT$1,220 to NT$1,270 on the back of 2% 2027-28E earnings hike.

### Page 4

BT substrate supply/demand gap at 11%/14% in 2027/28E on the back of solid memory demand

We introduce our 2026-28E BT substrate TAM forecast, expanding at a 32% 2026-28E 
CAGR to reach US$16.8bn in 2028E (up from US$7.4bn in 2025; Exhibit 1). This growth is 
primarily driven by solid memory demand in general servers (general servers will account 
for 32% of total BT TAM in 2028E, up from 21% in 2025; Exhibit 2), with increasing 
memory capacity/BT content per server. We expect the memory BT substrate TAM to 
outpace the broader BT substrate TAM with a 41% 2026-28E CAGR, accounting for 77% 
of total BT substrate TAM in 2028E (was 56% in 2025). 
On the other hand, we expect the capacity growth rate to decelerate vs. the previous 
upcycle in 2020-22 (6% 2026-28E CAGR vs. 9% 2020-22 CAGR), as key Asian substrate 
suppliers are placing greater focus on their ABF substrate business, and are hence 
scaling back their BT substrate capacity (including Unimicron, which held 8% market 
share in 2025). 
As such, we now expect the overall BT substrate supply/demand gap to reach 
1%/11%/14% in 2H26/2027/2028 (Exhibit 3), while major BT suppliers have started to 
reduce capacity from 2026, potentially leading to a supply shortage in 2H26. We expect 
BT capacity utilization rates (UTR) to trend up amid a supply-constrained environment 
(surpassing 100% from 2027E per GSe), providing a better BT pricing environment going 
forward. The BT substrate supply tightness since the start of the year has directly led to 
a 10% YTD increase in overall BT substrate pricing, and we believe pricing momentum 
for BT substrates will remain robust in 2H26-2028E. 
Accordingly, we forecast overall BT substrate prices will further increase by 
15%-20%/10%-15% in 2027/28E, benefiting BT substrates’ profitability/pricing for 
Taiwanese players (GMs ranged from 15%-27% in 2Q26 and are projected to increase to 
18%-34%/19%-36%+ in 2027/28E; Exhibit 4).

Solid growth in general server BT substrate TAM will support mid- to long-term BT 
market growth 
We expect general server BT substrate TAM to deliver a 42% 2026-28E CAGR and reach 
US$5.3bn in 2028E (up from US$1.6bn in 2025; Exhibit 8), which will account for 32% of 
total BT substrate TAM in 2028E (up from 21% in 2025). We expect general server 
memory BT substrate TAM to grow slightly faster at a 43% 2026-28E CAGR and reach

shipments (we expect general server shipments to register an 8% 2026-28E CAGR; see 
here; Exhibit 9) alongside increasing memory capacity/BT content per general server 
(Exhibit 10).

grow at a 28%/36% 2026-28E CAGR, reaching a combined 59mn units in 2028E (vs. 
26mn units in 2025). This supports our constructive view that general servers are the key 
driver of the current BT substrate upcycle, given the strong correlation between memory 
BT substrate TAM growth and general server CPU shipment growth.

### Page 8

Strong AI server shipment growth in 2026-28E will also support BT TAM expansion 
We expect AI server BT substrate TAM to deliver a 73% 2026-28E CAGR and reach 
US$1,476mn in 2028E (up from US$160mn in 2025; Exhibit 14), accounting for 9% of 
total BT substrate TAM in 2028E (up from 2% in 2025). We expect AI server memory BT 
substrate TAM to grow slightly faster at a 75% 2026-28E CAGR and reach US$1,428mn 
in 2028E (up from US$145mn in 2025), accounting for 9% of total memory BT substrate 
TAM in 2028E (up from 2% in 2025). This is driven by solid growth in AI server shipments, 
as we expect AI servers (8-GPU equivalent) and AI server racks (72-GPU equivalent) to 
register 17%/71% 2026-28E CAGRs (see here) with increasing memory capacity per 
server/per server rack. 
We expect GPU/CPU attach rates to move from 2x to 1.1-1.4x over time as agentic AI 
applications become mainstream, implying that each GPU deployment will require more 
server CPUs (see our US analyst Jim Schneider’s Intel initiation). Given that memory BT 
content in AI servers is primarily tied to server CPU count, if the GPU/CPU attach rate 
narrows from 2.0x to 1.1-1.4x, we estimate 1%/1%-2% upside to memory BT substrate 
TAM in 2027/28E. 
For AI server racks (72-GPU equivalent), BT content per rack continues to expand mainly 
due to increasing demand for more memory capacity, resulting in a 50% 2026-28E 
CAGR in BT content per AI server rack (Exhibit 15). A key upgrade is that Nvidia’s VR200 
will support higher DRAM capacity, implying a 100% increase in DRAM memory BT 
substrate content compared to GB200/GB300. We also note Nvidia is releasing a 
standalone Vera CPU rack designed for agentic AI, implying further demand upside for 
AI CPU shipments and memory BT substrates, as each Vera CPU rack incorporates 8x 
more DRAM memory BT substrate content compared to VR200.

### Page 9

Smartphones remain critical to BT demand with growth going forward tracking 
slower than the overall BT industry 
We expect overall smartphone BT substrate TAM to grow at a 24% 2026-28E CAGR 
(tracking slower than the overall BT industry CAGR of 32%) to reach US$6.5bn in 2028E 
(up from US$3.8bn in 2025), accounting for 39% of total 2028E BT TAM (down from 
51% in 2025). We expect smartphone memory BT substrate TAM to grow at a faster 36% 
2026-28E CAGR to reach US$2.4bn in 2028E (up from US$1.0bn in 2025), accounting 
for 19% of total memory BT TAM in 2028E (down from 24% in 2025). 
As mentioned in our previous update on the BT substrate industry (see here), we believe 
the previous upcycle (in 2020-22) was mainly driven by the proliferation of SiP/AiP 
designs that increased BT substrate content per device, especially for smartphones. 
While smartphones remain critical to overall BT demand, accounting for 51% of total 
2025 BT substrate TAM, we expect their contribution to moderate going forward 
(reaching 39% of 2028E BT TAM). This is driven by softer smartphone shipment volumes 
(we now forecast smartphone shipments at -10%/+2%/+2% YoY in 2026-28E; see here) 
and the fact that BT substrate content per smartphone will be mainly driven by higher 
memory BT substrate pricing (Exhibit 16) rather than other meaningful content value 
gains (memory will account for 46% of smartphone BT ASP in 2028E, up from 32% in 
2025). 
We expect 25%/26%/28% of overall smartphone shipments to be equipped with 
mmWave connectivity in 2026/27/28E (Exhibit 17), resulting in an 18% 2026-28E CAGR 
for smartphone AiP BT TAM to reach US$1,128mn in 2028 (up from US$713mn in 2025). 
For iPhones, mmWave connectivity is only available for models sold in the US 
(representing 35%-40% of total iPhone shipments per GSe). In the Android camp, 
Samsung’s flagship smartphones that support mmWave connectivity are also only for 
the US market.

Automotive BT consumption to steadily increase in coming years 
We expect automotive BT TAM to grow at a 34% 2026-28E CAGR and reach US$777mn 
in 2028E (up from US$317mn in 2025), which will account for 5% of total 2028E BT TAM 
(up from 4% in 2025). This stronger-than-industry growth is mainly driven by rising EV 
penetration going forward (EV penetration rate will increase from 14% in 2025 to 20% in 
2028E per our Japanese auto team; Exhibit 19) and the corresponding increase in 
electronics content per car, which requires more BT substrates. Furthermore, we believe 
rising autonomous driving penetration will result in an increase in electronic 
components/BT consumption per car.

### Page 14

We have seen BT substrate demand improve since the start of 2026 alongside a firmer 
pricing outlook, and we expect demand growth to consistently outpace supply in 
2026-28E (demand CAGR of 15% vs. supply CAGR of 6%). We believe industry utilization 
rates (UTR) will continue to trend up over the coming years (reaching 100%+ after 
2027E) due to slow supply growth going forward. 
Memory predominantly requires wire bonding chip scale package (WBCSP) technology, 
and this production capacity is not interchangeable with flip-chip chip-scale package 
(FCCSP) technology (where smartphone APs represent the biggest demand driver). As 
such, we believe memory BT substrate supply will remain tight through at least 1H28, as 
new dedicated memory capacity will only come online in 2028. Overall BT pricing has 
already risen by 10% YTD, partially driven by higher material costs (including substrate 
CCL, copper foil, gold, etc., which account for 60% of total BT substrate BOM; Exhibit 
26). Going forward, we believe the slow increase in BT substrate supply, especially 
WBCSP capacity, will be the key price hike driver rather than simply reflecting the 
material cost increase. Furthermore, BT substrates typically account for less than 5% of 
total memory BOM, providing ample room for further BT price hikes amid the rising 
memory pricing trend.

### Page 16

Optical module mSAP is seeing a widening S/D gap, prompting a shift of BT capacity to meet strong 
demand

We introduce our 2026-28E 800G+ optical module mSAP TAM forecast, expanding at an 
87% 2026-28E CAGR to reach US$7.1bn by 2028E (up from US$0.5bn in 2025; Exhibit 
28). This is based on our 800G+ optical module shipment estimates of a 48% 2026-28E 
CAGR (see here). The connectivity PCB within the 800G+ optical module requires mSAP 
technology, where several PCB makers have exposure to and are able to leverage their 
extensive mSAP experience from smartphone substrate-like PCB (SLP) production. 
On the other hand, major PCB and substrate suppliers are placing greater focus on this 
emerging business and pursuing proactive capacity expansion plans, especially ZDT and 
Compeq (the latter aims to ramp optical module mSAP capacity to reach monthly 
revenue of ~NT$3bn by end 2027 vs. less than NT$100mn as of 2Q26; see here). 
Although Unimicron was a front-runner in the 800G+ optical module mSAP business in 
2025 (29% market share in 2025 per GSe), the company is placing greater focus on its 
ABF substrate business, and hence leaving room for emerging peers to capture market 
share through aggressive expansion plans and technology leadership. 
We believe ZDT will become the market share leader in the optical module mSAP 
industry, as we expect the company to expand mSAP capacity by 5x over the next two 
years, positioning it well to gain share in a supply-constrained environment (our industry 
checks indicate mSAP is the most supply-constrained component in the 1.6T optical 
transceiver supply chain). Furthermore, the company currently holds an industry-leading 
yield of 90%+ for its optical module mSAP products. 
Overall, we expect the 800G+ optical module mSAP supply/demand gap to reach 
5%/25%/38% in 2026/27/28E (Exhibit 29). Although major mSAP suppliers continue 
to have proactive expansion plans, this appears insufficient to keep pace with strong 
demand. The 800G+ optical module mSAP tightness since the start of 2026 has directly 
led to a 20%-30% pricing increase YTD, and we believe the pricing outlook for 800G+ 
optical module mSAP will remain solid in 2027/28E.

### Page 23

Investment thesis, PT methodology and risks

Investment Thesis - Zhen Ding Technology 
Zhen Ding Technology (ZDT) is the global leading PCB supplier (top 1 in market share in 
sales) with 7-10% market share, and started its ABF substrate business from 2023 in 
Shenzhen, China; it has the highest ABF revenue exposure to the China market compared 
to all other ABF substrate suppliers. We believe ZDT will hold more than 40% ABF 
substrate market share in China by 2027E (up from 31% in 2024) and benefit the most 
from the strong and growing China ABF substrate market (53% 2025-27E CAGR) due to 
the increasing in-sourcing ratio for the AI computing ASIC/GPU market in China. We also 
expect the increasing foldable smartphone penetration rate in the long term to drive 
ZDT’s FPC TAM, and account for 39% of the company’s total revenue in 2027E. We see 
further revenue upside potential from foldable phones. We are Buy rated mainly due to 
our positive view on the company’s China ABF substrate business opportunity, which 
should not only drive ZDT’s revenue growth, but also improve its OPM level in the long 
term. We see upside for the stock price considering the company’s solid growth 
opportunity, and our TP implied P/E multiple is still lower than the company’s past 10+ 
year P/E average, which we believe is fair considering the potential upside from China 
ABF substrate market and increasing foldable phone penetration rate.  
Valuation: Our 12-month TP of NT$1,025 is based on a SOTP valuation methodology, 
including (1) 3.5x blended 2028E P/B for ABF & BT substrate, (2) 20x 2028E P/E for 
high-growth AI server/switch related PCB (in line with industry average), and (3) 8x 
2028E P/E for low-growth consumer electronics PCB (in line with industry downcycle 
average valuation). 
Key downside risks include: (1) weaker-than-expected iPhone demand or slower 
content upgrade, (2) delay in new substrate capacity or slower-than-expected yield rate 
ramp up progress, and (3) fiercer competition in China ABF market.

### Page 24

Investment Thesis - Kinsus
Kinsus is one of the key IC substrates suppliers globally with ~10% ABF/BT substrates
market share in 2024, with ~18% of total 2025 revenue contributed by its contact lens
subsidiary, Pegavision (6941.TW, Not Covered). With our view that the ABF substrate
shortage started from 2Q26 (ABF substrate return to supply demand balance from late
2025), we believe Kinsus’ performance will continue to improve compared with high-end

ABF peers considering the company’s increasing market share in the high-end ABF
substrate market; however, ~36% of the company’s revenue was contributed by BT
substrates in 2025 and 30% of the company’s ABF revenue is from non-LTA customers,
which we believe will continue to enjoy a better pricing outlook. As a result, we believe
Kinsus’ earnings outlook should continue to be strong (~118% 2026-29E CAGR), mainly
driven by better BT substrate and non-LTA ABF substrate pricing outlook, despite the

unfavorable demand outlook before 1H26. We believe Kinsus is an experienced
substrate supplier with a clear capacity expansion plan that can also upgrade its product
portfolio in the long term. We believe we are at the beginning of an industry upcycle,
making us positive on Kinsus’ stock performance over the next 12 months.
Price Target Risks and Methodology - Kinsus
Valuation methodology: Our 12-month target price of NT$1,515 for Kinsus is based on

19.5x 2028E P/E, which is +2.0x STDV higher than Kinsus’ past upcycle average P/E
(14.2x).
Key downside risks: (1) Slower-than-expected PC demand recovery, (2)
Slower-than-expected ABF substrates pricing upgrading outlook, (3)
Slower/Less-than-expected AI server orders from 2H26.
Investment Thesis - NYPCB
NYPCB manufactures and markets printed circuit boards (PCBs) and integrated circuit
(IC) substrates (including ABF and BT substrates). We are Buy rated on NYPCB

considering the company’s high exposure to BT substrates and non-LTA ABF substrate
(70%+ of total revenue), where we expect GM/OPM to increase significantly in the
coming quarters (from breakeven in OPM in 1H25 to 26/42% in 2026/27E) due to the
favorable substrate pricing outlook. Despite the company’s low exposure to high-end
ABF substrate industry (<20% of total revenue in 2025), we believe the expanding
demand from ASIC AI server and high-end switch IC demand will drive the company’s

high-end ABF revenue exposure to 40%+ by 2027E, suggesting better GM/OPM outlook
in the long term. In 2H25-2H26, we believe NYPCB will continue to benefit from stronger
ABF/BT pricing and should be a key beneficiary of the pricing uptrend considering the
company’s key focus on BT and non-LTA ABF substrate industries. Moreover, in the long
term (after 2026), we expect the ABF industry to return to supply tightness, and NYPCB’s
expanding high-end exposure will lead to a long-term earnings growth outlook (148%

2026-28E earnings CAGR). As a result, we see solid upside potential for NYPCB’s
earnings and stock performance.
Price Target Risks and Methodology - NYPCB
Valuation methodology: Our 12-month target price of NT$2,700 for NYPCB is based
on 21x 2028E P/E, which is +1.5x STDV higher than the past upcycle average P/E (13.3x).
Key downside risks: (1) Slower-than-expected PC demand recovery, (2)

Slower-than-expected ABF substrates pricing upgrading outlook, and (3)
Slower-than-expected NYPCB new high-end capacity qualification progress.
Investment Thesis - Unimicron Technology
Unimicron is a key supplier of ABF substrates (25% market share in 2025) and BT
substrates and also produces FPC/HDI/PCB products. We are Neutral rated on the
shares given long-term growth opportunity prospects: (1) over 70% of ABF shipments
are covered by long-term agreements (LTA), which limits pricing flexibility and makes

price hike less likely; (2) the company’s execution track record has been weaker than
peers; (3) capacity expansion plan remains slower than peers, constraining its ability to
capture incremental AI demand. The current ABF substrates market is seeing more
favorable supply demand outlook, and Unimicron is likely to see better pricing outlook
to reflect rising material costs in the coming quarters. AI ABF account for 10% of the
company’s total revenue, but the growth is likely to be slower than peers due to the

company continuing to lose market share to peers given its less favourable production
yield and slow capacity expansion. Overall, we are Neutral rated on Unimicron given our
outlook for limited upside potential, barring ASIC AI servers, despite our generally
positive view in the long term on its profitability trajectory.
Price Target Risks and Methodology - Unimicron Technology
Valuation methodology: Our 12-month target price of NT$1,270 for Unimicron is

based on 17.4x 2028E P/E, which is 2.7x STDV higher than the past upcycle average P/E
(10.3x).
Key upside/downside risks: (1) Slower/faster-than-expected PC demand recovery, (2)
Slower/faster-than-expected ABF substrates upgrading pace, and (3) Slower/faster
than-expected AI server PCB market share loss progress.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

outlook for 4Q26 thanks to AI ASIC contribution and better mix/pricing for
consumer products. On the back of solid operating leverage, we are also looking for

leverage. We lift our earnings projection for 2026/2027 /28 by 9%/ 25%/9%. We
maintain our Buy with TP$6,800 (30x of 2027 /28 EPS, from 35x previously, mainly
on recent macro concerns and concerns on AI capex beyond 2028).

### Page 7

Mediatek (2454.TW)
21 October 2026 Citi Research
7
Mediatek
Company description
Mediatek (MTK) is a fabless IC company that designs SoC for smartphone,
smart TV, and digital media devices. It also develops chips for connectivity,
networking, smart home gadgets, and automotive sectors. It was founded in
1997, is listed on the Taiwan Stock Exchange, and is headquartered in Taiwan.
It has sales and research subsidiaries in mainland China, Singapore, India,

the US, Japan, Korea, Denmark, and England. It primarily sells its products in
China and other emerging-market countries.
Investment strategy
We rate Mediatek shares as Buy. We expect Mediatek to ride on 5G migration
in emerging markets and market share gain of its high-end Dimensity series in
China market as well. In particular, we see strong growth potential from its AI
ASIC, NTN-satellite business in the long term. We believe solid fundamentals

and strong operating leverage will support Mediatek’s margins outlook.
Valuation
Our NT$6,800 target price (rounded) for Mediatek is based on 30x P/E to
2027 /2028 EPS, which is 1-std above its five-year average forward P/E given
the promising outlook from its AI ASIC business starting from 2H26. We
believe our target PE is also justified by Mediatek's global and regional peers'
trading at an average of c32x PER. AI ASIC development with CSPs should

provide more upside from 2027 onwards. We also see Mediatek as well
prepared for 6G and 6G+NTN Satellite communication. Despite lukewarm
smartphone overall deamand, we expect Mediatek to benefit from 5G
migration in emerging markets and its Dimensity series should also gain
shares in flagship models. Non-smartphone business is also seeing
stabilizing TV and Io T demand with Mediatek outgrowing the overall market
on its better technology in WiFi 7 and connectivity.

Risks
Key downside risks that could prevent the shares from reaching our target
price include: 1) weaker-than-expected growth from AI ASIC business; 2)
more severe competition in the smartphone segment; 3) slower overall
demand related to macroeconomic risks, especially for smartphone demand;
and 4) further price hikes from foundries.
Prepared for Kevin Lu

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.
Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst

expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment
rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient

valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating
and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of

information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price
chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and

investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the
expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.

Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:
Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in

reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is
moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.
Privately held companies:
The legal entities through which Citi conducts its business do not permit investment research recommendations, ratings,

not guaranteed. Private companies are generally not subject to reporting obligations, which may result in less publicly available
information. The information contained in this report may differ from information a private company may subsequently make
public or otherwise share with investors. Continuation of publishing private company reports or private company sections
within reports is at Citi's discretion, in accordance with internal policies.

The legal entities employing the authors of this report are listed below (and their regulators are listed further herein). Non-US
research analysts who have prepared this report (i.e., all research analysts listed below other than those identified as employed
by Citigroup Global Markets Inc.) are not registered/qualified as research analysts with FINRA. Such research analysts may not

may not be subject to the FINRA Rule 2241 restrictions on communications with a subject company, public appearances and
trading securities held by a research analyst account.

TAIWAN COOPERATIVE SECURITIES 
INVESTMENT CONSULTANT

2

營運展望 
1. 2H26 營運動能來自 AMD Helios 伺服器專案開始放量：針對 Meta、Oracle 與 
OpenAI 等客戶的 AMD Helios 專案，已由去年的研發與小量送樣階段，於今
年正式啟動大量生產。儘管受限於保密協議無法透露單一客戶細節，但整體專
案規模與出貨量將迎來顯著成長。2026 年上半年營收約 41.0 億元，第三季單
季貢獻即達 40.3 億元 ， 推升前三季累計營收至 81.3 億元 。 受惠下半年強勁拉
貨，全年營收預估將突破百億大關並創下歷史新高，年增率上看五成。 
2. 與 NVIDIA 深化儲存專案合作，共同開發下次世代應用：跟 NVIDIA 除延續
多年來在人工智慧資料處理器 (Data Processing Unit, DPU AI) 儲存市場合作，
更於今年  GTC 大會深化與  NVIDIA 策略夥伴關係。雙方正緊密圍繞最新  
Vera Rubin 平台，共同開發下一代鍵值快取 (Knowledge Base Cache, KB Cache)  
在內容管理系統 (Content Management System , CMS ) 的應用。與 NVIDIA 次
世代合作案正處聯合開發階段，將聚焦提供高階 AI 儲存完整解決方案，其市
場份額與營收貢獻將隨 NVIDIA 的市場推廣逐步發酵。 
3. 2027 年既有業務穩健成長 ， 新興 AI 業務加速拓展：在三大事業部同步成長 ，
以及歐洲 、 東南亞 、 印度與中國等非北美市場大幅貢獻的帶動下 ， 預期 2027 年
仍將延續五成的強勁營收成長幅度。預期標準品、AI 儲存與 CSP 三大事業群
的營收年增率將呈現均衡且穩定成長。其中，標準品於各區域市場皆持續貢獻
穩定營收，AI 儲存受惠於整體應用規模擴增趨勢而由專責團隊推動成長，CSP 
專案則依既有進度維持穩健的營收佔比 。公司整體毛利率穩定維持在 25 ~ 30% 
區間，未來各項產品銷售與營運規劃，也將持續以穩住此毛利率水準作為首要
目標。 
4. 三大事業部門業務能見度佳，明年營運獲利可創新高：三大事業部同步成長，
Server & Storage 標準品業績規模持續擴大，出貨量顯著成長。AI Storage 出貨
快速攀升，掌握重點客戶成長商機。CSP 專案持續拓展，AMD Helios 進入大
規模量產階段 。合庫預估 2026 年營收 122.8 億元 、+63.4% YoY， 毛利率 28.2%。
營業利益 18.7 億元、+112.8% YoY，營業利益率 15.2%，歸屬母公司稅後淨利 
17.1 億元，每股盈餘 39.73 元。 
5. ESG (營邦在 2026 年上櫃公司治理評鑑結果為 81 ~ 100%)：為實踐企業社會
責任，並促進經濟、社會與環境生態之平衡及永續發展，營邦制定「永續發展
實務守則」 與 「永續資訊管理作業程序」 ，將永續做為公司營運決策的一環。透
過檢視治理、環境與社會的管理架構，營邦運用永續策略促進創新，以符合平
衡環境、社會及公司治理發展之國際趨勢，並透過企業公民擔當，提升國家經
濟貢獻 ， 改善員工 、 社區 、 社會之生活品質 ， 促進以永續發展為本的競爭優勢 。

### Page 1

삼성바이오로직스 (207940/KS) 
3Q26 Pre: 파업과 환율 부담에도 컨센 부합 
2026-10-06

제약/바이오. 이선경 / seonkyoung.lee@sks.co.kr / 3773-9089

매수(유지) 
목표주가: 2,000,000 원(유지) 
현재주가: 1,354,000 원 
상승여력: 47.7%

Signal: 파업과 환율 부담에도 컨센 부합 전망  
Key: 5 공장 매출 본격화, 미국 공장 매출 기여에 따른 성장 기대   
Step: 단기 불확실성보다는 중장기적 성장 방향성에 주목

3Q26 Pre: 파업과 환율 부담에도 성장지속, 컨센 부합 전망   
26년 3분기 연결기준 매출액은 1조 3,741억원(+9.3%YoY, +4.0%QoQ), 영업이
익은 6,098억원(-3.7%YoY, +4.0%QoQ, OPM 44.4%)으로 컨센서스 매출액 1
조 3,450억원과 영업이익 6,063억원에 부합하는 실적을 전망한다.  
5월 파업 영향으로 약 20개 배치 생산분(약 1,500억원)에 대한 원가 손실이 인식되
고 3분기 평균 환율이 전분기 대비 약 5% 하락했음에도 불구하고, 3분기부터 매출로 
인식되는 5공장 PPQ 배치 및 미국 공장의 매출 기여로 지속적인 성장이 예상된다. 약 
1,500억원 규모의 생산 차질분은 고객사와 일정 조율을 통해 출하될 예정으로 이에 
따른 4분기 매출 성장을 기대해 볼 수 있다.   
신규 수주 회복과 모달리티 확장에 주목 
시장에서 우려했던 신규 수주는 관세 이슈로 고객사의 컨택이 감소하면서 올해 저조했
으나 4 월 Section 232 결과 발표 이후 논의가 회복 단계에 있어 연내 추가 신규 수주 
가능성은 열려 있다.  
항체 시장의 경쟁심화와 차세대 모달리티 확장에 대응하기 위해 결정한 폴리펩타이드
의 인수는 연내 마무리될 예정이다. 폴리펩타이드의 2028 년 목표 EBITDA 마진은 
25%로 동사 대비 낮은 상황이나, 펩타이드 기반 CDMO 시장 성장에 따른 마진 개선
도 가능하다. 현재 임단협, 환율, 미국 공장 및 폴리펩타이드 인수에 따른 회계 영향 등 
불확실성이 큰 상황이나, 수주 회복과 5 공장 매출 인식 본격화, 미국 공장 및 멀티 모
달리티 확장 등 중장기 성장 방향성에 주목할 필요가 있다.

## Document 231: [402340] SK스퀘어(402340) 하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후가 더 기대SK스퀘어(402340) 하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후가 더 기대하이닉스 자사주 소각_31fbe439

Source PDF: all report pdf/[402340] SK스퀘어(402340) 하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후가 더 기대SK스퀘어(402340) 하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후가 더 기대하이닉스 자사주 소각_31fbe439.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-09-30
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

SK 스퀘어 (402340/KS) 
하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후가 더 기대 
예정보다 빠른 SK 하이닉스 자사주 매입 
SK스퀘어의 자회사인 SK하이닉스는 지난 8월 20일부터 11월 19일까지 자사주 40
조원(2,407만주, 3.3%)을 매입 예정이다. 9월 29일까지 1,715만주, 예정 매입수량 
대비 71.3%를 매입 완료하였다. 매입 초기 매일 65만주 매입하다가 9월 10일부터는 
60만주를 매입하고 있는데, 이러한 추세가 유지된다면 10월 중순경 자사주 매입이 마
무리될 예정이다. 자사주 매입 후 소각 예정으로 소각이 완료되면 SK스퀘어의 SK하아
닉스에 대한 지분율은 20.0%에서 20.7%로 상승하게 된다.

자사주 매입 이후 추가적인 주주환원에 대한 기대 
SK하이닉스는 지난 8월 19일 정책기간(‘25년~’27년) 동안 발생하는 누적 FCF의 
50% 이상을 주주환원 하는 주주환원 정책을 발표한 바 있다. 26년 SK하이닉스의 예
상 FCF는 164조원(컨센서스 기준)으로 50%를 주주환원 할 경우 자사주 매입 금액
(40 조원)을 제외한 주주환원 재원은 42 조원이다. 잔여 주주환원 재원이 모두 배당으
로 지급된다면 SK 하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후 SK 스퀘어의 지분율은 20.7%이므로 
SK스퀘어의 배당수입도 8.7조원에 달한다. 지난 3월 SK스퀘어는 ‘26년~’28년 경
상배당수입의 30% 이상을 주주에게 환원하는 정책을 발표했다. 보수적으로 30%만 
가정하더라도 SK스퀘어의 배당재원은 2.6조원(현 시가총액 대비 1.8%)이다. SK스퀘
어는 연초 주주총회에서 배당가능이익 확보 차원에서 자본준비금 5 조 8,900억원을 
이익잉여금으로 전입한 바 있다. 이는 26년 재무제표가 확정되는 27년 3월 이후 배당
가능이익으로 편입되므로 SK스퀘어의 주주환원 확대가 기대된다.

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 185 만원(유지) 
SK스퀘어의 순자산 가치 중 98% 이상이 SK하이닉스 지분가치이다. SK하이닉스는 
자사주 매입 이후 17.7% 주가가 상승했으나, SK 스퀘어 주가는 11.4% 상승했다. SK
하이닉스 자사주 매입·소각은 SK 스퀘어의 보유 지분가치 상승뿐 아니라 지분율도 상
승하기 때문에 자사주가 소각될 경우 SK스퀘어의 주가가 탄력적으로 움직일 가능성이 
높다. SK스퀘어 현재 NAV 대비 할인율은 43.7%, 12MF PBR 1.4배로 7월 이후 주
가 조정으로 밸류에이션 매력도 높아졌다. 투자의견 매수와 목표주가 185만원을 유지
한다.

2026-09-30

지주회사/통신서비스/ESG. 최관순 / ks1.choi@sks.co.kr / 3773-8812

## Document 244: Anthropic IPO招股书八大看点：增速逆天，但风险也是明摆着的

Source PDF: all report pdf/Anthropic IPO招股书八大看点：增速逆天，但风险也是明摆着的.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-27
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Anthropic 招股书披露多项亮点：企业客⼾规模庞⼤，收⼊超百万美元客⼾逾 1900 家；截⾄
2026 年 6 ⽉，收⼊同⽐增⻓ 1368% ，远超计算成本增速；经营现⾦流转正；全部 7 位创始⼈
仍在职。公司同时坦承⻛险，台积电供应集中及内存芯⽚短缺构成重⼤隐患。 10 ⽉ ARR 数
据将在修订版⽂件中补充披露。
摘要：
前沿 AI 模型公司 Anthropic 正式迈出上市关键⼀步，这将是 AI 领域迄今规模最⼤的 IPO 。
华尔街⻅闻提及， Anthropic 周⼀向美国证券交易委员会递交 S-1 招股说明书， 318 ⻚的⽂件披露
了其商业模式、⻛险因素及财务状况，消息提振周⼀ AI 相关芯⽚股⾛势。
招股书在第 194 ⻚预留了 10 ⽉年度经常性收⼊（ ARR ）的空⽩占位符，待修订版 S-1 中补充披
露。
资深科技财经作者 Tae Kim 发表专栏指出，这⼀安排本⾝即释放积极信号 ——若 10 ⽉ ARR 数据不
佳， Anthropic 完全没有义务将其纳⼊修订⽂件，主动预留意味着公司对该数据有信⼼。

9 ⽉ 22 ⽇， Anthropic 已发布性能⼤幅提升的 Opus 5.5 模型，另⼀款新模型 Fable 5.5 亦即将推
出。分析认为，新模型上线有望带动公司业务加速，并在 IPO 前进⼀步推升 ARR 表现。
Tae Kim 援引招股说明书，总结了⽂件中最引⼈注⽬的 8 个看点：
看点 1 ：股票代码是 ANT
我们已申请将我们的  A 类普通股在纳斯达克全球精选市场上市，股票代码为 “ANT” 。
看点 2 ： Anthropic ⼤⼒宣传其企业⽂化和员⼯留存率
作为联合创始⼈及领导者，我们⼒求实现内部彻底的坦诚，并营造了⼀种⿎励各层级员⼯之间进
⾏活跃、开放辩论的企业⽂化。
我们的⾸席执⾏官达⾥奥通常每两周都会在公司全体员⼯⾯前发表讲话，坦诚地阐述他正在考虑
的决策以及⾏业现状；其他联合创始⼈及领导者也经常在各⾃领域内进⾏类似的分享。
员⼯们会在⾯向全公司的开放论坛上，就我们复杂使命所带来的各种权衡取舍展开讨论。这种开
放的⽂化是我们⼈才密度⾼、⼈才留存率始终位居⾏业前列的原因之⼀。

我们七位创始⼈⾄今仍在公司⼯作，这使得 Anthropic 成为唯⼀⼀家保留了全部创始团队的⼤型
⼈⼯智能公司。
看点 3 ：企业级应⽤正经历着惊⼈的指数级收⼊增⻓。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

企业客⼾采⽤我们产品的速度和深度有⼒地证明了  Claude 在⼈⼯智能领域的前沿地位。截⾄
2026 年  9 ⽉  30 ⽇，我们的客⼾群包括：
•过去 12 个⽉收⼊超过 10 万美元的客⼾超过 11900 家； • 过去 12 个⽉收⼊超过 100 万美元的客⼾超
过 1900 家；以及
•过去 12 个⽉收⼊超过 100 万美元的客⼾约 250 家。

### Page 3

看点 4 ：这些⾃我强化的改进循环是他们的竞争优势
改进循环。 Claude 在企业级部署中的应⽤，加深了我们对客⼾最为关注的难题、边缘案例和特
定领域挑战的理解。我们根据客⼾在⽣产环境中使⽤我们产品的反馈，改进我们的模型、平台和
应⽤程序。这些改进促成了更⼴泛的部署和应⽤，从⽽推动下⼀轮改进。
投资循环。企业级应⽤拓展带来的收⼊，有助于我们为  Claude 的改进提供所需的计算、研究和
⼈才⽀持。
看点 5 ：存储芯⽚供应和台积电晶圆分配都是很⼤的⻛险
⼈⼯智能⼯作负载所需的专⽤芯⽚，特别是⾼性能⼈⼯智能加速器和内存芯⽚，由少数⼏家制造
商⽣产。先进芯⽚的⽣产极其复杂，需要尖端的技术⼯艺。全球⼤部分最先进的芯⽚制造能⼒集
中在中国台湾，主要集中在台积电（ TSMC ）。这种地域集中带来了重⼤⻛险，包括易受地震、
台⻛等⾃然灾害的影响，以及亚洲地缘政治紧张局势。
此外，由于对先进芯⽚制造的需求，台积电有时⽆法完全满⾜市场对芯⽚制造能⼒的需求，因
此，我们并⾮总能及时获得⾜够的芯⽚。
因此，任何对中国台湾或台积电先进芯⽚供应的限制或延误都将对我们的业务、财务状况和经营

业绩产⽣不利影响。此外，⾼性能⼈⼯智能加速器的关键组件 —— ⾼带宽内存，以及计算基础设
施中使⽤的其他⾼性能内存产品，主要由 SK 海⼒⼠、三星电⼦和美光科技⽣产。这些制造商都
在努⼒扩⼤产能以满⾜需求。然⽽，这些努⼒能否迅速实现充⾜的供应以满⾜需求，尤其是在⼤
型客⼾或竞争对⼿获得独家供应承诺或以其他⽅式预订了制造商的⼏乎所有可⽤产能的情况下，
这⼀点⽆法保证。
看点 6 ：推理成本每年都⼤幅下降
即便我们加快了研发和产品迭代速度，并扩⼤了内部  Claude 的使⽤范围，每美元计算成本带来
的收 ⼊ 也 显 著 增 ⻓ 。 例 如 ， 截 ⾄  2026 年  6 ⽉  30 ⽇ 的 三 个 ⽉ 内 ， 我 们 的收 ⼊ 同 ⽐ 增 ⻓
1368% ，⽽同期计算和基础设施⽀出仅增⻓了  300% 。
在满⾜不断增⻓的客⼾需求的同时，我们还凭借  Mythos 5 、 Fable 5 和  Opus 5 等领先的新模
型，在前沿研究领域取得了重⼤进展，并推出了  Claude Cowork 和  Claude Design 等新产品。

## Document 269: Capital Market Outlook (2026-09-14)

### Page 1

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook
September 14, 2026
All data, projections and opinions are as of the date of this report and subject to change.
IN THIS ISSUE
Macro Strategy—Superintelligence and Superabundance in the Next Decade:
Rapid advances in Artificial Intelligence (AI) have prompted prominent architects of
the new economy to conclude the Singularity is arriving ahead of schedule as signs
of its effects proliferate in the multitude of new AI models running in the increasing

number of data centers driving one of the biggest investment booms in U.S. history.
Superintelligence that surpasses collective human intelligence has long been one of the
predictions of futurists analyzing the implications of the Singularity. Superabundance is
one of the results of Superintelligence predicted by those who have written about the
consequences of this major acceleration in technological innovation. The early arrival of
these radical forces for change poses imminent challenges for society and investors that

will shape the next decade in critical ways.
In particular, the heightened uncertainty about how this new era plays out increases the
utility of a well-diversified approach to wealth management in our view.
Market View—Elevated Debt Supply Is Testing the Market's Appetite: The recent
rise in Treasury yields reflects a confluence of factors, including Federal Reserve (Fed)
policy uncertainty, deteriorating fiscal dynamics, resilient economic growth, persistent

inflation concerns and a record wave of debt issuance from both the U.S. Treasury and
corporate borrowers, particularly in the long end of the curve. Importantly, we view this
as a period of market adjustment rather than a cause for alarm.
Episodes of debt pressures can be a natural consequence of elevated supply, in addition
to other external factors and could be resolved through higher yields that help balance
supply and demand. While interest rate volatility may remain elevated and long-term

yields could settle at structurally higher levels, today's combination of attractive nominal
and real yields presents a potential opportunity for investors to extend beyond cash and
short-duration holdings and move closer to strategic duration targets.
The Ticker Tape—Top Questions Answered Directly by Our Chief Investment
Officer (CIO).
• What are the markets actually telling us?

### Page 2

2 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Superintelligence and Superabundance in the Next Decade
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
While some academics have claimed the Singularity is still off in the distant future, or even
unlikely at all, those on the front lines of AI model development have recently asserted that it
has already begun. For example, Elon Musk has pointed to recent issues with AI models going

outside their proscribed limits, including hacking external systems and completing previously
unsolved math problems. Over a year ago, Sam Altman wrote, "We are past the event horizon; the
takeoff has started. Humanity is close to building superintelligence..." In short, the rapid evolution
of these models has convinced knowledgeable observers that the Singularity has begun.
Superintelligence is the primary driver of the Singularity as exponential advances in AI ultimately

create an artificial Superintelligence that is much smarter and faster than the collective
intelligence of humanity. As such, it can solve practical problems that remain unsolved by humans
and do it in a timely fashion. Superabundance is the logical implication of Superintelligence
as it works to create a world where economic goods, services and energy are essentially free
because of the omniscience implied by the most efficient processing of unlimited information by

superintelligent models. In essence, Superintelligence could mimic the Creation.
Some aspects of Superabundance include automated production of desired output that
eliminates most physical labor, clean energy and synthetic materials that eliminate resource
scarcity, and increased longevity as Superintelligence is applied to maintaining physical health
via, for example, synthetic organ replacement and personalized illness treatments. Obviously,
Superabundance pulls the rug out from under traditional economics, which is the study of how

scarce resources are allocated. While the "scarcity" vanishes in this new world, the allocation issue
remains and is likely to become the focus of political debate. In particular, the question of how
jobs and incomes fit into a superabundant world is likely to become a bigger and bigger issue.
The trend of technology replacing more and more of the labor share of income is already decades
old and dates back to the beginnings of digital technology. That trend has picked up speed

with the increasing profit share of output the new technologies created. The ratio of income
to the number of workers is orders of magnitude higher for the big new technology companies
compared to the old economy companies of the past. This means the owners of capital
(shareholders) are receiving a bigger share of the economic pie as high-margin, high-productivity
companies based in the new technology become a bigger part of the economy. This trend is

accelerating. That is a direct implication of the movement toward and through the Singularity.
It means the issue of income inequality, which has always been on society's radar screen, will
become bigger in the years ahead unless there are wise policies to share the superabundance
of the near future. Some countries are better prepared for this future than others. Norway,
for example, has built a sovereign wealth fund (SWF) over the past 25 years that represents a

substantial share of capital income for each of its citizens. In the U.S., with its widespread equity
culture, thanks to Individual Retirement Accounts and 401K plans, capital income is more widely
distributed compared to Europe with its much more debt-based capital markets. In any event,
widespread equity ownership is one way to deal with a superabundant economy where AI has
significantly reduced the labor share of income.
Demographics add to the natural evolution from labor income to capital income as societies

become wealthier. Aging societies of retirees by definition depend on capital income more than
labor income. This transition of the economy to higher margins and faster earnings growth is
the fortunate accompaniment to the need for more income from capital as societies become
older. The longevity trend naturally accelerates this process, which is the logical result of
technological progress raising productivity and living standards. People live longer in wealthy

societies, as subsistence constraints on life spans diminish while lifestyle and mental health
constraints become more the issue. How lifestyles and mental health evolve in a world where
work becomes less necessary is already showing signs of their increased importance for the
future of public health.
The current accelerating profits cycle reflects this impact of new technology on the relative
returns to labor and capital. The trend so far has been relatively slow compared to what's

coming if Superintelligence and Superabundance are the outcomes the Singularity promises as
increasingly seems likely. Because much financial analysis relies on past cycles the accelerating
Investment Implications
Rapid innovation, political upheaval
and increasing creative destruction
raise the difficulty of valuing
longer duration assets as we saw
earlier this year in the software
space. Higher-risk premia are a
likely result. On balance, this favors

Value-oriented assets over Growth.
Higher real yields in a strong capital
spending environment reinforce this
preference.

### Page 3

3 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
profits and rising margins of the current economy have naturally focused attention on what
happens when conditions revert to the mean of lower margins and more historically normal
profit growth. Looked at through this lens, Equities appear overvalued and destined to correct
bubble-like conditions. The Singularity, however, implies "this time is different," the four most
dangerous words in investing. Yet, the slowly evolving shift toward capital income already under

way is an early warning sign of bigger things to come as these trends are turbo-charged to warp
speed while the economy passes through the Singularity. Seen this way, a super-cycle in profits,
margins and stock values seems logical.
A revamping of the social safety net to address the conditions implied by Superabundance
is already a hot topic in Silicon Valley. Various proposals for a universal basic income (UBI)
are being bandied about. Typically, UBI refers to the government giving everyone an income

sufficient to meet some living standard. This automatically implies a big role for government in
the new economy. Given the fourth-turning breakdown in the political process and governance,
alternatives that avoid the government sector might also be considered. For example, another
implication of the Singularity is an accelerating accumulation of wealth fueling a growing supply
of altruistic and philanthropic potential that arguably could be administered more efficiently by

the private sector than the government where widespread allegations of corrupt administration
at all levels have proliferated since the pandemic. Given the growing wealth pie and the diversity
of social priorities among its owners addressing more social needs through philanthropy is a
likely option for the superabundant economy. In any event, the relative roles of government and
the private sector are necessarily an issue to be hashed out in this revolutionary time.

More glimmers of the UBI-like movements have popped up recently. As mentioned, Norway's
SWF is an example of how a well-administered government has been preparing for this future.
In the U.S. government sponsored tax-advantaged savings accounts from birth have been
instituted with private donations being added to the program. Governments, including the U.S.,
have increasingly been buying stakes in private investments for various purposes with the result

that the focus solely on sovereign debt can be a misleading indicator of fiscal fragility.
Japan is a significant example of the difference between gross government debt and net
government debt after subtracting government owned assets. Its gross debt is around 279% of
its gross domestic product (GDP) which is much higher than any other major economy. However,
Japan government assets total around 192% of GDP , including domestic and foreign Equities,

bonds, and loans. As a result, its net debt is less than 100% of GDP and more in line with other
major economies. As one of the most aged societies in the world, Japan points the way toward
how societies can cope with longevity. Government ownership of a broad array of higher yielding
global assets funded with low sovereign debt interest rates appears to be a path the U.S.
Treasury is also starting to pursue in managing its debt burden.
The main risks to the uptopian promise of Superabundance are the dystopian possibilities

that would follow from poor social decision-making. Around the world, different governments
will follow different approaches to navigating the unprecedented challenges posed by the
Singularity. As the U.S. celebrates its 250-year anniversary, it is in the midst of its fourth "fourth
turning." These are the intense political upheaval periods that demographers and historians have
documented in every fourth generation. The Revolutionary War, the Civil War, and the Great

Depression and World War II mark the first three "fourth turnings." The Singularity is a major
marker for the current fourth turning.
The relative success of the U.S. in transitioning through the previous major turning points in
history provides hope for a utopian rather than a dystopian outcome. Other countries have not
transited these challenges so well for such an extended period which helps explain how the
U.S. has become the most prosperous country on earth. Nevertheless, past performance is no

guarantee of future success. As always, each generation faces new challenges to overcome, or not.
For investors, the Singularity seems to be behind an unusually strong earnings cycle that could
potentially last much longer than past history would suggest. That's positive for Equities and
risk assets in general. The extra risks associated with this rapid transformation argue for well
diversified holdings as creative destruction on steroids seems a plausible outcome in a world of

warp speed change.

### Page 4

4 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Elevated Debt Supply Is Testing the Market's Appetite
Chief Investment Office, Fixed Income Strategy Team
For much of the past two decades, rising debt levels appeared to carry few
consequences. Federal deficits expanded and corporate borrowing increased, yet
Treasury yields generally trended lower and credit spreads stayed remarkably tame.
Today, the backdrop may be changing.

Federal borrowing needs continue to grow as deficit spending remains elevated. Net
interest expense has surpassed $1 trillion annually and now rivals total U.S. defense
spending. On this front there appears no end in sight as the Congressional Budget
Office projects that the fiscal 2026 shortfall will widen to roughly $2.1 trillion. Investors
are increasingly focused on not only the level of debt outstanding, but also the pace at
which new debt is being issued—with some questioning about the sustainability of this

situation over the longer term.
This phenomenon is not limited to Treasurys, as the issuance glut extends to corporate
debt. Investment-grade (IG) corporate issuance is running roughly 40% above last year
and is on pace for a record year. 1 While demand for Fixed Income remains healthy, the
sheer volume of new debt being brought to market is beginning to test investor capacity,
particularly at the long end of the Treasury curve and within certain sectors.

Exhibit 1: Low Yields Post-2008/2009 GFC* Have Fueled a Borrowing Binge.

Hyperscalers Are Competing for Long-Duration Capital. A significant driver of
the surge in corporate issuance has been the hyperscalers. The largest technology
companies are financing an unprecedented wave of AI-related capital spending, leading
to nearly $280 billion of debt issuance year-to-date. 2 Many of these issuers carry high
credit ratings and have ramped up issuance of longer-maturity bonds. As a result, they
are increasingly competing with long-dated Treasurys for the same pool of institutional

capital, including insurance companies, pension funds, sovereign wealth funds and
duration-oriented Fixed Income investors—creating a crowding-out effect amongst high-
quality Fixed Income.
Fortunately, this is a case of pressure, not a credit crisis. Recent weakness in long-dated
Treasurys reflects a classic supply-and-demand imbalance and importantly is not a
solvency crisis for the U.S. government or high-quality hyperscalers. Investors are being

asked to absorb a growing volume of net new issuance from both the U.S. Treasury and
corporate America at the same time.
Debt Pressure and a Normalization, Not a Fiscal Reckoning. The rise in long-end
yields during August reflects this dynamic. The 30-year Treasury yield moved above
5.3%, reaching levels not seen since 2007. Fiscal concerns likely played a role, but we

### Page 5

5 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
believe heavy supply, sticky inflation, reduced Fed demand and increased competition for
investor capital were equally important contributors.
And importantly, the market's adjustment mechanism is already working. Higher Treasury
yields and somewhat wider credit spreads are attracting buyers and helping clear the growing
supply of bonds. This is a natural normalization and is how markets restore equilibrium.

Recent Treasury auctions continue to attract demand consistent with historical norms, and
investor appetite for new IG issuance has remained robust. These are not characteristics
typically associated with a loss of confidence in U.S., or corporate, creditworthiness.
A New Yield Regime. For Fixed Income investors, the more important implication
is that a 4% to 5% 10-year Treasury yield may represent the new normal. In an
environment of very strong underlying economic growth and unprecedented volumes of

debt issuance from both the public and private sectors, higher yields may be required to
attract sufficient capital amid a more competitive landscape for yield.
We continue to view both nominal and real yields as attractive and believe investors
should remain focused on achieving strategic duration targets. While rate volatility may
persist, today's yield levels provide a potential opportunity to seek attractive income that
has been largely absent for much of the past decade.

The Bigger Picture: Growth Still Matters More Than Debt Levels. None of this dismisses
the long-term fiscal challenges facing the U.S. Debt levels are rising, interest costs
continue to grow, and mandatory spending increasingly consumes a larger share of the
federal budget. However, history shows that debt levels alone do not determine interest
rates. Between 2007 and 2020, U.S. debt rose dramatically even as the 10-year Treasury
yield fell from roughly 5% to below 1%. What ultimately matters is the interplay between

short-term interest rates, debt levels, economic growth, inflation and investor demand.
For instance, nominal GDP growth and the 10-year yield tend to be correlated, outside
of recessions—we believe recent rate increases partly reflect this strong macro
backdrop. Moreover, economic growth is running above long-end yields, supporting the
sustainability of rising government debt.

The U.S. continues to benefit from several structural advantages: the dollar remains
the world's reserve currency, Treasurys remain the foundation of the global financial

system, and U.S. capital markets are still the deepest and most liquid in the world. Those
advantages continue to support demand for U.S. assets and underpin sovereign credit
quality even as borrowing needs grow.
The bottom line, in our view, is that even though the recent rise in long-end yields catches
many headlines, there is no need to panic. Instead, the recent repricing reflects periodic
episodes of debt pressure rather than a fundamental loss of confidence in U.S. credit. With

borrowing demand from both Washington and corporate America likely to remain elevated,
bond prices may continue to serve as the market's release valve. The consequence is likely
a world of structurally higher long-term yields, not a sovereign debt crisis.

6 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What are the markets actually telling us?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investmen t Officer
When the markets speak loudly we should listen. There are often clues in the internal trends
within the markets that help us determine how far into an economic cycle we are or what drivers
of growth are most impactful. In addition, we can also find clues that help with determining

the micro picture. In other words, what industries are the most likely to benefit, from a
profitability standpoint is concerned, as the growth picture unfolds? Currently, the markets have
a megaphone, in our view. The year-to-date sharp outperformers have been consistent and
should continue to advance. These include commodities, including copper and oil, Emerging
Markets, Japan in the Developed Markets, Small-cap relative to Large-cap shares, Value versus

Growth, and more cyclically-oriented companies versus more defensive. This current consistent
outperformance informs us that we are still early- to mid-cycle in a supply-led expansion. We are
still early in the build out of the supply that is needed for the natural demand that is there. New
infrastructure is needed to handle the new economy that is progressing and quickly innovating.
This infrastructure needs commodities, all forms of energy, battery storage, minerals, metals,

recycled water, gases, chemicals, and all the compute and cloud capacity, including memory
and other components to handle the path toward mass tokenization. Moreover, the buildup of
government debt in the advanced economies as fiscal deficits widen is forcing large institutional
investors (including central banks and other government entities) to diversify their reserve
bases. This has increased the interest in gold holdings. Countries that have a vast amount of

natural resources, especially oil and gas, are in high demand by investors, which is obvious when
examining year-to-date outperformers. The bottom line is that the markets are telling us that we
are still early- to mid-cycle in this massive buildout, which will require a wide collection of hard
assets, Infrastructure (old and new), land, and within Equities, a more diversified approach to
exposure—particularly areas that are more cyclical- and Value-oriented. We expect this view to

extend itself well into the foreseeable future if not years.
In Fixed Income, it may seem more complex, but it is actually easier to understand what the
markets are telling us, in our opinion. The backup in
yields is “real yield” driven, which is more due
to higher nominal growth than to concerns of long-run inflation. This is evident in the Treasury
inflation protection market, break-evens in general and the ratio of Treasury yields relative to
other Go

vernment bond markets in the developed world. However, rising oil and gas prices and
inflation gauges that are still above the preferred level are usually the excuses for yields backing
up. Deficit concerns are a more plausible reason f
or yields rising but the main driver, in our view,
is still abo
ve average nominal growth. The yield curve and rates in general are still adjusting to
this new economic landscape that is very different than those of the most recent past.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

Bloomberg Commodity Index designed to provide liquid and diversified exposure to commodities via futures contracts. Is calculated on an excess return basis and reflects commodity
futures price movements.
MSCI Emerging Markets Index captures large and mid cap representation across 24 Emerging Markets countries. With 1,205 constituents, the index covers approximately 85% of the free
float adjusted market capitalization in each country.
MSCI Japan Index is designed to measure the performance of the large and mid cap segments of the Japanese market.

MSCI Pacific ex Japan Index captures large and mid cap representation across 4 of 5 Developed Markets (DM) countries in the Pacific region (excluding Japan). With 93 constituents, the index
covers approximately 85% of the free float-adjusted market capitalization in each country.
MSCI All-Country World Index is a premier benchmark representing global equity performance, covering large and mid-cap stocks across 23 developed and 24 emerging markets.

MSCI World Index ex U.S. captures large and mid cap representation across 22 of 23 Developed Markets countries--excluding the United States..
MSCI USA Momentum Index is based on MSCI USA Index, its parent index, which captures large and mid-cap stocks of the U.S. market. It is designed to reflect the performance of an equity
momentum strategy by emphasizing stocks with high price momentum, while maintaining reasonably high trading liquidity, investment capacity and moderate index turnover.

U.S. Large-cap Growth/Russell 1000 Growth Index measures the performance of the large-cap growth segment of the U.S. equity universe. It includes those Russell 1000 companies with
relatively higher price-to-book ratios, higher I/B/E/S forecast medium term (2 year) growth and higher sales per share historical growth (5 years).
U.S. Small-cap/Russell 2000 Index measures the performance of the small-cap segment of the U.S. Equity universe.
MSCI USA High Dividend Yield Index is designed to reflect the performance of equities in the MSCI USA Index (excluding REIT s) with higher dividend income and quality characteristics than

average dividend yields that are both sustainable and persistent.
U.S. Mid Cap/Russell Midcap Index measures the performance of the mid-cap segment of the U.S. Equity universe. The Russell Midcap Index is a subset of the Russell 1000® Index. It
includes approximately 800 of the smallest securities based on a combination of their market cap and current index membership.
MSCI USA Value Index captures large and mid-cap U.S. securities exhibiting overall value style characteristics.

MSCI USA Equal Weighted Index represents an alternative weighting scheme to its market cap weighted parent index, the MSCI USA Index. At each quarterly rebalance date, all index
constituents are weighted equally, effectively removing the influence of each constituent’s current price.
MSCI USA Minimum Volatility Index aims to reflect the performance characteristics of a minimum variance strategy applied to the large and mid-cap USA equity universe.

risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.

## Document 312: First Impressions_ NZ Consumers Price Index Q3 2026 and change of OCR call (2026-10-27)

### Page 1

#inflation #newzealand
First Impressions: NZ Consumers Price Index Q3 2026 and change
of OCR call
By Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
09:19 October 22 2026 Share
Consumer prices rose 0.7% in the September quarter and 3.7% y/y. Core inflation
pressures are elevated and market pricing is primed for a 25bp OCR hike next week, which
we now expect.
Consumers Price Index, September quarter 2026 – key stats

### Page 2

Headline inflation
Quarterly change: +0.7% (prev: +1.5%)
Westpac forecast: +0.7%
RBNZ: +0.8%
Market median: +0.8%, range +0.5% to +1.2%

Annual change: +3.7% (prev: +4.1%)
Westpac forecast: +3.7%, RBNZ: +3.9%, Market: +3.8%

Non-tradables
Quarterly change: +1.0% (prev: +0.6%)
Westpac forecast: +1.3%
Annual change: +3.3% (prev: +3.4%)

Tradables
Quarterly change: +0.2% (prev: +2.7%)
Westpac forecast: -0.2%
Annual change: +4.3% (prev: +4.9%)

Consumer prices rose 0.7% in the September quarter. That saw the annual inflation rate easing to
3.7%, down from 4.1% in the year to June. 
Today’s result was in line with our forecast, but lower than the RBNZ’s forecast for 3.9% annual
inflation. 
However, under the surface, the details were more concerning. Measure of core inflation have taken a
step higher, with several closely watched measures now at or slightly above the top of the RBNZ’s
target band. 
We suspect that today’s result will leave the market continuing to price a strong chance of another
OCR hike at next Wednesday’s policy review. In that light, we think the RBNZ will regard this as the
path of least resistance, and we now expect that they will deliver (previously we expected that they
would pause and assess, before hiking at the December MPS).

What contributed to inflation in the September quarter?
Underpinning September’s 0.7% rise in consumer prices were some big price movements in both
directions. 
The largest move in prices this quarter was actually a sharp fall in fuel costs. Fuel prices aren’t low by
any stretch of the imagination. However, average prices for both petrol and diesel over the September
quarter were lower than in the June quarter when prices rocketed higher in the wake of the Middle East

### Page 3

conflict. In terms of specifics, the average price for 91 unleaded was 6.5% lower in September than in
the June quarter, while diesel prices were close to 16% lower. Those moves took 0.4ppts off inflation
this quarter. 
While prices at the pump weren’t as high as they were in the June quarter, fuel costs are still painfully
high. And that’s been bleeding through into higher prices for other goods and services. Of note, the
September quarter saw large increases in passenger transport costs, with international airfares up 14%
and domestic airfares up nearly 8%. 
The September quarter also saw the annual increases in local council rates (up 6.2%) and charges for
services like water supply (up 7.5%). That continues the run of large increases seen in recent years.
Lastly, the past few months saw a 1.0% rise in food prices, largely as a result of the seasonal rise in
prices for fresh vegetables. 
On the downside, we’ve seen continued softness in housing rents, which are the largest component of
the CPI, accounting for 11.2% of the basket. With low population growth and ample supply, rents were
flat in the September quarter and have effectively been flat since the start of this year. In annual terms,
rental inflation is the lowest it’s been in more than two decades, with regions like Wellington seeing
outright falls.

Annual and core inflation 
The annual inflation rate dropped back to 3.7% in the September quarter, down from 4.1% in the year
to June and the highest level in two years.
Looking under the surface, prices in the domestically oriented non-tradables group rose 1.0% over the
quarter, less than expected. That saw annual non-tradables inflation nudging down to 3.3% (from 3.4%
in the year to June). 
Non-tradables inflation has continued to run at above-average rates over the past year. That’s despite
the softness in economic growth and the labour market. In large part, that’s due to the continued
sizeable increases in administered prices, such as electricity charges which rose 9.3% over the past
year. 
However, prices in other areas have also been firm. Non-tradables excluding housing and utilities costs
(which also omits local council rates) was 3.4% for the year. In seasonally adjusted terms, quarterly
non-tradables inflation has been tracking around 0.8% for most of the past year. It was noted that in
sectors like construction, higher materials costs are adding to prices. Feedback from business we've
heard indicates that those higher materials costs are likely related to high oil prices. 
On the imported front, lower fuel costs saw tradable prices rising by just 0.2% over the September
quarter (though above our forecast for a small fall). That saw annual tradables inflation dropping back
to 4.3%, down sharply from the 4.9% rise in the year to June. 
While fuel costs have been a key driver for tradables inflation, under the surface, other imported
inflation pressures are looking firm. Excluding food and fuel costs, tradables prices were up 2.7% over
the past year. That’s the highest level in three years. 
With big swings in fuel costs, and concern that this is spilling over into operating costs more generally,
a key focus for the RBNZ is what’s been happening to measures of core inflation (which smooth
through quarter-to-quarter swings in individual prices and instead track the underlying trend in

### Page 4

Related articles
inflation). 
On this front we did some see worrying signs. Several closely watched measures of core inflation have
picked up, and now sit at the top of (or slightly above) the RBNZ target band. In terms of specifics, we
saw: 
Reinforcing this picture of firm underlying inflation pressures, a growing number of goods have been
increasing in price. That could be an early indication that businesses, even those with a limited
exposure to fuel costs, are starting to factor in expectations of continued high inflation when setting
prices.

Outlook 
While today’s result was lower than the RBNZ assumed in their September MPS projections, the
firmness in core inflation will give the RBNZ pause for thought ahead of their policy review on 28
October. Furthermore, with a sharp rise in petrol prices in recent weeks, September’s easing in annual
inflation is likely to be just a temporary reprieve. We expect headline inflation to reaccelerate back over
4% before the end of this year, and it’s set to remain above the RBNZ’s current forecast for most of
2027. 
Market pricing and views have coalesced around an October hike which we now suspect the MPC will
see as the path of least resistance. Core inflation measures are now close to or above 3%, which will
add fuel to arguments from the MPC hawks. We will have more detail in our MPR Preview later today,
including on the implications for the shape of the tightening cycle moving forward.

CPI ex-fuel, food and household energy: +2.9% yr (vs +2.5% previously)

TAKING STOCK

The Reserve Bank of India (RBI) has adopted a 
multi-layered defence strategy to support the 
rupee this year.

This has entailed a combination of outright 
intervention in spot/ forwards, regulatory 
action, liquidity steps, administrative measures 
and moves to curb import -related dollar 
demand.

The rupee has appreciated by 1.8% against the 
dollar in the past five days since recent 
measures took effect, paring YTD depreciation 
to 5.5% . High oil prices, weakness in capital 
inflows, a firm dollar , tariffs/ geopolitical 
worries, energy , and risk -off sentiments have 
kept the INR under pressure since last year.

India: Degrees of rupee defence

Radhika Rao, Senior Economist

### Page 2

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            2

ECHOES OF 2013 WITH FEW 
DIFFERENCES

Comparisons with 2013 are apt, though 
the backdrop differs.  As for the 
catalysts, markets reacted to the US 
Fed’s taper announcement in 2013, 
which pushed US yields higher  and led 
global investors to reduce exposure to 
vulnerable EM economies. As a result, 
capital inflows weakened, exposing 
funding mismatch es for countries, 
including India, that were running wide 
current account deficits.

In 2026, markets face the spectre of 
elevated oil prices, geopolitical 
uncertainty, global competition for 
capital, a firm dollar ( DXY appreciated 
+3.8% YTD) , and US inflation/ deficits 
which have settled at levels much higher 
than pre -pandemic years. This has 
pushed up UST yields, and the US Fed 
kicked off its rate-hike cycle in Sep26.

Against this backdrop, the RBI is 
pursuing the same objective , i.e., to 
ensure rupee stability, but the nature of 
the battle differs. The defence strategy 
has shifted from ‘crisis management’ 
mode in 2013 , due to weaker domestic 
macroeconomic balances (a wide current 
account gap, high inflation, a lower 
reserve buffer, and wide fiscal deficits) , 
to a ‘terms-of-trade shock and impact of 
a firm dollar’ in 2026.

Flashpoints are  nonetheless similar, 
with reliance on portfolio financing , 
leaving the external account vulnerable 
to shifts in global risk sentiment. 
Portfolio outflows have emerged as the 
primary transmission channel through 
which external shocks have translated 
into INR depreciation pressure , 
compounded by high energy prices and a 
firm dollar.

FX PLAYBOOK

While the response framework is similar 
between 2013 and this year, the broad 
sequence and nature of macroprudential 
measures differ. This year’s multi-
layered rupee defence toolkit has 
included:

-Direct FX defence  by way of 
intervention in the spot and forwards 
market. As of Aug26, the net forward 
dollar liabilities ballooned to $200bn, 
rising by ~$63bn vs . the month before . 
Recent sell/buy FX swaps are expected 
to lower this stock in the coming months.

-Attracting dollar inflows  by way of 
special swap facilities for FCNR deposits, 
and offshore borrowings , apart from 
liberalising taxes around debt inflows,

### Page 3

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026

amongst others. We discussed this here,
